   54:  *
   55:  *  If there was no match, NULL is returned. Otherwise, a pointer to an
   56:  *  initialized cpu struct is returned.
   57:  */
   58: struct cpu *cpu_new(struct memory *mem, struct machine *machine,
   59:         int cpu_id, char *name)
   60: {
   61: 	char *cpu_type_name;
   62: 	char tmpstr[30];
   63: 
   64: 	if (name == NULL) {
   65: 		debugmsg(SUBSYS_CPU, "", VERBOSITY_ERROR, "cpu name = NULL?");
   66: 		return NULL;
   67: 	}
   68: 
   69: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(cpu_type_name = strdup(name));
   70: 
   71: 	struct cpu *cpu = (struct cpu *) zeroed_alloc(sizeof(struct cpu));
   72: 
   73: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(cpu->path = (char *) malloc(strlen(machine->path) + 15));
   74: 	snprintf(cpu->path, strlen(machine->path) + 15,
   75: 	    "%s.cpu[%i]", machine->path, cpu_id);
   76: 
   77: 	cpu->memory_rw  = NULL;
   78: 	cpu->name       = cpu_type_name;
   79: 	cpu->mem        = mem;
   80: 	cpu->machine    = machine;
   81: 	cpu->cpu_id     = cpu_id;
   82: 	cpu->byte_order = EMUL_UNDEFINED_ENDIAN;
   83: 	cpu->running    = 0;
   84: 
   85: 	/*  Create settings, and attach to the machine:  */
   86: 	cpu->settings = settings_new();
   87: 	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "cpu[%i]", cpu_id);
   88: 	settings_add(machine->settings, tmpstr, 1,
   89: 	    SETTINGS_TYPE_SUBSETTINGS, 0, cpu->settings);
   90: 
   91: 	settings_add(cpu->settings, "name", 0, SETTINGS_TYPE_STRING,
   92: 	    SETTINGS_FORMAT_STRING, (void *) &cpu->name);
   93: 	settings_add(cpu->settings, "running", 0, SETTINGS_TYPE_UINT8,
   94: 	    SETTINGS_FORMAT_YESNO, (void *) &cpu->running);
   95: 
   96: 	cpu_create_or_reset_tc(cpu);
   97: 
   98: 	struct cpu_family *fp = first_cpu_family;
   99: 
  100: 	while (fp != NULL) {
  101: 		if (fp->cpu_new != NULL) {
  102: 			if (fp->cpu_new(cpu, mem, machine, cpu_id, cpu_type_name))
  103: 				break;
  104: 		}
  105: 
  106: 		fp = fp->next;
  107: 	}
  108: 
  109: 	if (fp == NULL) {
  110: 		debugmsg(SUBSYS_CPU, "", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
  111: 		    "unknown cpu type '%s'", cpu_type_name);
  112: 		/*  cpu was allocated with zeroed_alloc() (mmap), so it
  113: 		    must be munmap()ed, not free()d; cf. cpu_destroy().  */
  114: 		munmap((void *)cpu, sizeof(struct cpu));
  115: 		return NULL;
  116: 	}
FILE src/cpus/cpu.c
  546: 
  547: /*
  548:  *  add_cpu_family():
  549:  *
  550:  *  Allocates a cpu_family struct and calls an init function for the
  551:  *  family to fill in reasonable data and pointers.
  552:  */
  553: static void add_cpu_family(void (*family_init)(struct cpu_family *), int arch)
  554: {
  555: 	struct cpu_family *fp;
  556: 
  557: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(fp = (struct cpu_family *) malloc(sizeof(struct cpu_family)));
  558: 	memset(fp, 0, sizeof(struct cpu_family));
  559: 
  560: 	family_init(fp);
  561: 
  562: 	fp->arch = arch;
  563: 	fp->next = NULL;
  564: 
  565: 	/*  Add last in family chain:  */
  566: 	struct cpu_family* tmp = first_cpu_family;
  567: 	if (tmp == NULL) {
  568: 		first_cpu_family = fp;
  569: 	} else {
  570: 		while (tmp->next != NULL)
  571: 			tmp = tmp->next;
  572: 		tmp->next = fp;
  573: 	}
  574: }
  575:

  577: /*
  578:  *  cpu_init():
  579:  *
  580:  *  Should be called before any other cpu_*() function.

  582:  *  This function calls add_cpu_family() for each processor architecture.
  583:  *  ADD_ALL_CPU_FAMILIES is defined in the config.h file generated by the
  584:  *  configure script.
  585:  */
  586: void cpu_init(void)
  587: {
  588: 	ADD_ALL_CPU_FAMILIES;
  589: }

FILE src/file/file_raw.c
   38:  *
   39:  *  Loads a raw binary into emulated memory. The filename should be
   40:  *  of the following form:     loadaddress:filename
   41:  *  or    loadaddress:skiplen:filename
   42:  *  or    loadaddress:skiplen:pc:filename
   43:  */
   44: static void file_load_raw(struct machine *m, struct memory *mem,
   45: 	char *filename, uint64_t *entrypointp)
   46: {
   47: 	FILE *f;
   48: 	int len, sign3264;
   49: 	unsigned char buf[16384];
   50: 	uint64_t entry, loadaddr, vaddr, skip = 0;
   51: 	char *p, *p2;
   52: 
   53: 	/*  Special case for 32-bit MIPS:  */
   54: 	sign3264 = 0;
   55: 	if (m->cpus[0]->cpu_family->arch == ARCH_MIPS && m->cpus[0]->is_32bit)
   56: 		sign3264 = 1;
   57: 
   58: 	p = strchr(filename, ':');
   59: 	if (p == NULL) {
   60: 		fprintf(stderr, "\n");
   61: 		perror(filename);
   62: 		exit(1);
   63: 	}
   64: 
   65: 	loadaddr = vaddr = entry = strtoull(filename, NULL, 0);
   66: 	p2 = p+1;
   67: 
   68: 	/*  A second value? That's the optional skip value  */
   69: 	p = strchr(p2, ':');
   70: 	if (p != NULL) {
   71: 		skip = strtoull(p2, NULL, 0);
   72: 		p = p+1;
   73: 		/*  A third value? That's the initial pc:  */
   74: 		if (strchr(p, ':') != NULL) {
   75: 			entry = strtoull(p, NULL, 0);
   76: 			p = strchr(p, ':') + 1;
   77: 		}
   78: 	} else
   79: 		p = p2;
   80: 
   81: 	if (sign3264) {
   82: 		loadaddr = (int64_t)(int32_t)loadaddr;
   83: 		entry = (int64_t)(int32_t)entry;
   84: 		vaddr = (int64_t)(int32_t)vaddr;
   85: 		skip = (int64_t)(int32_t)skip;
   86: 	}
   87: 
   88: 	f = fopen(strrchr(filename, ':')+1, "r");
   89: 	if (f == NULL) {
   90: 		perror(p);
   91: 		exit(1);
   92: 	}
   93: 
   94: 	fseek(f, skip, SEEK_SET);
   95: 
   96: 	/*  Load file contents:  */
   97: 	while (!feof(f)) {
   98: 		size_t to_read = sizeof(buf);
   99: 
  100: 		/*  If vaddr isn't buf-size aligned, then start with a
  101: 		    smaller buffer:  */
  102: 		if (vaddr & (sizeof(buf) - 1))
  103: 			to_read = sizeof(buf) - (vaddr & (sizeof(buf)-1));
  104: 
  105: 		len = fread(buf, 1, to_read, f);
  106: 
  107: 		if (len > 0)
  108: 			m->cpus[0]->memory_rw(m->cpus[0], mem, vaddr, &buf[0],
  109: 			    len, MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS);
  110: 
  111: 		vaddr += len;
  112: 	}
  113: 
  114: 	debug("RAW: 0x%" PRIx64" bytes @ 0x%08" PRIx64,
  115: 	    (uint64_t) (ftello(f) - skip), (uint64_t) loadaddr);
  116: 
  117: 	if (skip != 0)
  118: 		debug(" (0x%" PRIx64" bytes of header skipped)",
  119: 		    (uint64_t) skip);
  120: 
  121: 	debug("\n");
  122: 
  123: 	fclose(f);
  124: 
  125: 	*entrypointp = entry;
  126: 
  127: 	n_executables_loaded ++;

FILE src/core/memory.c
  758:  *  store_64bit_word():
  759:  *
  760:  *  Stores a 64-bit word in emulated RAM.  Byte order is taken into account.
  761:  *  Helper function.
  762:  */
  763: int store_64bit_word(struct cpu *cpu, uint64_t addr, uint64_t data64)
  764: {
  765: 	unsigned char data[8];
  766: 	if ((addr >> 32) == 0)
  767: 		addr = (int64_t)(int32_t)addr;
  768: 	data[0] = (data64 >> 56) & 255;
  769: 	data[1] = (data64 >> 48) & 255;
  770: 	data[2] = (data64 >> 40) & 255;
  771: 	data[3] = (data64 >> 32) & 255;
  772: 	data[4] = (data64 >> 24) & 255;
  773: 	data[5] = (data64 >> 16) & 255;
  774: 	data[6] = (data64 >> 8) & 255;
  775: 	data[7] = (data64) & 255;
  776: 	if (cpu->byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN) {
  777: 		int tmp = data[0]; data[0] = data[7]; data[7] = tmp;
  778: 		tmp = data[1]; data[1] = data[6]; data[6] = tmp;
  779: 		tmp = data[2]; data[2] = data[5]; data[5] = tmp;
  780: 		tmp = data[3]; data[3] = data[4]; data[4] = tmp;
  781: 	}
  782: 	return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem,
  783: 	    addr, data, sizeof(data), MEM_WRITE, CACHE_DATA);
  784: }
  785:

  793:  */
  794: int store_32bit_word(struct cpu *cpu, uint64_t addr, uint64_t data32)
  795: {
  796: 	unsigned char data[4];
FILE src/core/memory.c
  882:  *  load_64bit_word():
  883:  *
  884:  *  Helper function. Emulated byte order is taken into account.
  885:  */
  886: uint64_t load_64bit_word(struct cpu *cpu, uint64_t addr)
  887: {
  888: 	unsigned char data[8];
  889: 
  890: 	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem,
  891: 	    addr, data, sizeof(data), MEM_READ, CACHE_DATA);
  892: 
  893: 	if (cpu->byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN) {
  894: 		int tmp = data[0]; data[0] = data[7]; data[7] = tmp;
  895: 		tmp = data[1]; data[1] = data[6]; data[6] = tmp;
  896: 		tmp = data[2]; data[2] = data[5]; data[5] = tmp;
  897: 		tmp = data[3]; data[3] = data[4]; data[4] = tmp;
  898: 	}
  899: 
  900: 	return
  901: 	    ((uint64_t)data[0] << 56) + ((uint64_t)data[1] << 48) +
  902: 	    ((uint64_t)data[2] << 40) + ((uint64_t)data[3] << 32) +
  903: 	    ((uint64_t)data[4] << 24) + ((uint64_t)data[5] << 16) +
  904: 	    ((uint64_t)data[6] << 8) + (uint64_t)data[7];
  905: }

  923: 	}

 1201:  *  flags should be one of
 1202:  *	INVALIDATE_PADDR  INVALIDATE_VADDR  or  INVALIDATE_ALL
 1203:  *
 1204:  *  In addition, for INVALIDATE_ALL, INVALIDATE_VADDR_UPPER4 may be set and
 1205:  *  bit 31..28 of addr are used to select the virtual addresses to invalidate.
 1206:  *  (This is useful for PowerPC emulation, when segment registers are updated.)
 1207:  *
 1208:  *  In the case when all translations are invalidated, paddr doesn't need
 1209:  *  to be supplied.
 1210:  *
 1211:  *  NOTE/TODO: When invalidating a virtual address, it is only cleared from
 1212:  *             the quick translation array, not from the linear
 1213:  *             vph_tlb_entry[] array.  Hopefully this is enough anyway.
 1214:  */
 1215: void DYNTRANS_INVALIDATE_TC(struct cpu *cpu, uint64_t addr, int flags)
 1216: {
 1217: 	int r;
 1218: #ifdef MODE32
 1219: 	uint32_t
 1220: #else
 1221: 	uint64_t
 1222: #endif
 1223: 	    addr_page = addr & ~(DYNTRANS_PAGESIZE - 1);
 1224: 
 1225: 	/*  fatal("invalidate(): ");  */
 1226: 
 1227: 	/*  Quick case for _one_ virtual addresses: see note above.  */
 1228: 	if (flags & INVALIDATE_VADDR) {
 1229: 		/*  fatal("vaddr 0x%08x\n", (int)addr_page);  */
 1230: 
 1231: #ifdef MODE32
 1232: 		DYNTRANS_INVALIDATE_TLB_ENTRY(cpu, addr_page, flags);
 1233: #else
 1234: 		// In principle, there can be multiple entries, and they must
 1235: 		// be invalidated based on the bits that matter. For example,
 1236: 		// using vaddr 0x00A0000012345678 and 0x00B000001234567c
 1237: 		// actually point to the same physical page, and if then
 1238: 		// the virtual address 0x0000000012345000 is to be invalidated,
 1239: 		// then both of those should be invalidated!
 1240: 		int n = 0;
 1241: 		for (r=0; r<DYNTRANS_MAX_VPH_TLB_ENTRIES; r++)
 1242: 			if (cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.vph_tlb_entry[r].valid &&
 1243: 			    (cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.vph_tlb_entry[r].vaddr_page
 1244: 			    & cpu->vaddr_mask) == (addr_page & cpu->vaddr_mask)) {

The numeric enumeration caught an error in my previous update: `0xfffffffd80000000` is **outside** K0SEG. The abort can fire with a zero base; it does not block the nonzero-base walk. Enumerating the PCB flags also confirms that a nonzero PTBR can shift to a zero base.

  113:  *  zeroed_alloc():
  114:  *
  115:  *  Allocates a block of memory using mmap(), and if that fails, try
  116:  *  malloc() + memset(). The returned memory block contains only zeroes.
  117:  */
  118: void *zeroed_alloc(size_t s)
  119: {
  120: 	void *p = mmap(NULL, s, PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE,
  121: 	    MAP_ANON | MAP_PRIVATE, -1, 0);
  122: 
  123: 	/*  #175: (Codex/Fable) mmap failure is MAP_FAILED, not NULL  */
  124: 	if (p == MAP_FAILED) {
  125: #if 1
  126: 		fprintf(stderr, "zeroed_alloc(): mmap() failed. This should"
  127: 		    " not usually happen. If you can reproduce this, then"
  128: 		    " please contact me with details about your run-time"
  129: 		    " environment.\n");
  130: 		exit(1);
  131: #else
  132: 		CHECK_ALLOCATION(p = malloc(s));
  133: 		memset(p, 0, s);
  134: #endif
  135: 	}

FILE src/core/memory.c
  530:  *  memory_paddr_to_hostaddr():
  531:  *
  532:  *  Translate a physical address into a host address. The usual way to call
  533:  *  this function is to make sure that paddr is page aligned, which will result
  534:  *  in the host _page_ corresponding to that address.
  535:  *
  536:  *  Return value is a pointer to the address in the host, or NULL on failure.
  537:  *  On reads, a NULL return value should be interpreted as reading all zeroes.
  538:  */
  539: unsigned char *memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(struct memory *mem,
  540: 	uint64_t paddr, int writeflag)
  541: {
  542: 	void **table;
  543: 	int entry;
  544: 	const int mask = (1 << BITS_PER_PAGETABLE) - 1;
  545: 	const int shrcount = MAX_BITS - BITS_PER_PAGETABLE;
  546: 	unsigned char *hostptr;
  547: 
  548: 	table = (void **) mem->pagetable;
  549: 	entry = (paddr >> shrcount) & mask;
  550: 
  551: 	/*  printf("memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(): p=%16" PRIx64
  552: 	    " w=%i => entry=0x%x\n", (uint64_t) paddr, writeflag, entry);  */
  553: 
  554: 	if (table[entry] == NULL) {
  555: 		size_t alloclen;
  556: 
  557: 		/*
  558: 		 *  Special case:  reading from a nonexistant memblock
  559: 		 *  returns all zeroes, and doesn't allocate anything.
  560: 		 *  (If any intermediate pagetable is nonexistant, then
  561: 		 *  the same thing happens):
  562: 		 */
  563: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
  564: 			return NULL;
  565: 
  566: 		/*  Allocate a memblock:  */
  567: 		alloclen = 1 << BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK;
  568: 
  569: 		/*  printf("  allocating for entry %i, len=%i\n",
  570: 		    entry, alloclen);  */
  571: 
  572: 		/*  Anonymous mmap() should return zero-filled memory,
  573: 		    try malloc + memset if mmap failed.  */
  574: 		table[entry] = (void *) mmap(NULL, alloclen,
  575: 		    PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE, MAP_ANON | MAP_PRIVATE, -1, 0);
  576: 		/*  #175: (Codex/Fable) mmap failure is MAP_FAILED, not NULL  */
  577: 		if (table[entry] == MAP_FAILED) {
  578: 			CHECK_ALLOCATION(table[entry] = malloc(alloclen));
  579: 			memset(table[entry], 0, alloclen);
  580: 		}
  581: 	}
  582: 
  583: 	hostptr = (unsigned char *) table[entry];
  584: 
  585: 	if (hostptr != NULL)
  586: 		hostptr += (paddr & ((1 << BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK) - 1));
  587: 
  588: 	return hostptr;
  589: }

codex
The source allows a guest `CALL_PAL` to reach `swpctx`, but that alone does not prove the offset defect occurs during a boot: the overwritten root can lead to a failed walk. I found no successful Alpha boot documented in the allowed files. The decisive observation is a guest access reaching the successful walk return, with a nonzero offset, and then consuming the page-base address.

**VERDICT: UNSETTLED for an actual Alpha guest boot. The offset defect is real, but neither LIVE boot impact nor LATENT/unreachable status is established. The PTBR hinge contains another defect.**

This was a read-only review of the requested fork and scoped files; I did not execute a guest boot.

**Q3: `apcb_ptbr` can become nonzero through implemented guest execution—but it receives the wrong value.**

CPU allocation initially zeroes it. Alpha machine setup supplies zero as the initial level-one-table PFN in `a1`. The only explicit runtime assignments to `apcb_ptbr` are in the implemented `PAL_OSF1_swpctx` handler:

- Line 278 assigns `new_ptbr`, loaded from the incoming PCB.
- Line 282 immediately overwrites it with `new_flags`.
- Translation caches are invalidated afterward.

There is no guest memory translation between those assignments. Consequently, the value available to subsequent walks is **the incoming PCB flags**, not its PTBR. [`swpctx` implementation](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c:229)

I enumerated all combinations of the two flag bits defined in the supplied Alpha header:

| Incoming PCB flags | PTBR after `swpctx` | `(uint64_t)(PTBR << 13)` |
|---|---:|---:|
| Neither | `0` | `0` |
| FEN | `1` | `0x2000` |
| PME | `0x4000000000000000` | `0` |
| FEN + PME | `0x4000000000000001` | `0x2000` |

Thus **nonzero PTBR is not precisely the hinge: nonzero shifted `base` is**. A watchpoint stopping at the intermediate assignment can also give misleading evidence. Inspect the state after the handler finishes. [Flag definitions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/alpha_cpu.h:74)

**Q1: Alpha incompleteness does not establish that the walk is unreachable.**

The fork registers AlphaBook 1, AlphaServer 4100, DEC 3000/300, and EB164. Alpha ELF loading and CPU startup exist, and the real instruction decoder dispatches `CALL_PAL` to the handler above. This is an implemented guest execution path, with no requirement for a new machine description. [Machine registration](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/machines/machine_alpha.c:236), [instruction decode](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr.c:839), [Alpha executable startup](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/emul.c:638)

However, I found **no documented successful Alpha OS boot** in the scoped material. The documentation explicitly describes Alpha support as barely working. Moreover, no implemented guest path sets the processor’s user-mode bit from reset: the status writes only change IPL, while `rti`, `retsys`, and `urti` have names but no execution cases. Enumerating IPL values `0–7` leaves the user-mode bit clear in every case. That blocks the expected transition into userspace, but does not establish that earlier kernel execution never walks tables. [Documentation](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/doc/TODO.html:716), [status writes](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c:328)

The missing evidence is whether an actual guest reaches `swpctx` with flags producing a nonzero base **and subsequently supplies a valid three-level walk**. Entering the walker and failing its first lookup would not demonstrate this offset defect.

Several premises need qualification:

- **Seven TODO occurrences: confirmed**, at lines **50, 121, 127, 142, 148, 164, 170**.
- **The explicit abort is conditional.** Numeric evaluation confirms `0xfffffffd80000000` is outside K0SEG. It can abort a kernel access with zero `base`; with nonzero `base`, execution bypasses that abort and enters the walk. [Early returns](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_alpha.c:61)
- **The offset arithmetic is confirmed.** I enumerated all **8,192 offsets**: the walk expression produces offset zero throughout, differing from the offset-preserving address for **8,191** offsets. The masking paths preserve offsets; exhaustive enumeration also confirmed preservation throughout all three bootstrap alias regions.
- **“Every access” is too broad.** Slow-path accesses consume the incorrect physical offset, but cached Alpha loads/stores calculate `addr & 8191` themselves. Subsequent cache hits can therefore access the correct within-page bytes. [Slow-path copy](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:728), [cached access](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c:183)

**Q2: the smallest decisive observation is one completed guest data access.**

Use the existing **EB164** description (`alpha` / `eb164`, default `21164PC`) with the Alpha guest image being evaluated. A host-debugger observation should establish:

1. A real guest load/store reaches the translator through Alpha’s memory path.
2. It reaches **`memory_alpha.c:168`**, after all three validity checks, with a nonzero virtual page offset.
3. The caller completes the access using the returned page-base address.

That establishes LIVE impact for that guest execution. A PTBR watchpoint or walker-entry breakpoint alone does not.

I found no source barrier making a guest-instruction witness impossible on the existing description. A standalone guest diagnostic could establish guest reachability; establishing impact during a particular OS boot still requires observing that boot. I cannot name a demonstrated working Alpha OS boot from the allowed files.

Two further findings:

- `swpctx` writes the old context into **`a0`**, although the supplied ABI wrapper returns **`v0`**. This independently contradicts the handler’s contract. [Handler](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c:250), [ABI wrapper](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/alpha_cpu.h:449)
- The fork’s claim that a failed Alpha walk causes the caller to raise a fault is unsupported by this path. The translator returns zero, the memory worker returns failure, and the Alpha data instruction abandons the access without delivering an Alpha exception. [Failed walk](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_alpha.c:173), [data-instruction handling](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_instr_loadstore.c:68)
